Drug-repurposing candidates for premature hair greying

run 20260802-220955 · created 2026-08-02T12:09:55+00:00 · 83 hypotheses · rounds 6/6

Disclaimer: This report is a research-prioritization artifact generated by an automated hypothesis-triage loop. It is not medical advice, and no candidate listed here is endorsed for clinical use. All hypotheses require expert review and experimental validation.

Meta-review synthesis

WHAT THE LOOP ACTUALLY CONVERGED ON, AND WHY THE ANSWER CHANGED IN THE LAST ROUND For five rounds this run converged confidently on a topical redox arm led by melatonin, supported by tetrahydrocurcumin, N-acetylcysteine and sulforaphane, with a prostaglandin melanogenesis-stimulation arm and a sympathetic-stress-axis (timolol) arm alongside it. Three successive meta-reviews ratified that structure; metareview_3 put H064, H065, H066, H040 and H067 forward as top picks, two of which were melatonin and methionine arms. That convergence was an artefact of the review protocol, not of the evidence. Rounds 1-5 verified only the citations each hypothesis chose to present. No reviewer was ever required to ask whether the proposed agent has a documented effect in the opposite direction - whether it is, in fact, a depigmenting agent. A contra-evidence requirement was added to the reviewer harness before round 6 and applied retrospectively to 30 hypotheses. The outcome: 19 DOCUMENTED opposite-direction, 7 POSSIBLE, 4 CLEAN. The clean four are H037, a lab panel that cannot have a pigmentary direction; and H040, H050 and H051, the alpha-MSH biomimetic peptides. Nineteen of thirty proposals for a repigmentation indication were, on their own published pharmacology, more likely to lighten hair than to darken it. The single most useful way to state the damage: the run's own top-ranked stack was self-antagonising. Melatonin blocks alpha-MSH-induced tyrosinase accumulation (PMID 7556159, external). A reader following this run's ranked table for five rounds would have built a regimen in which the leading agent pharmacologically cancels the only arm that survived audit, at the same receptor pathway. Independently, the beta-blocker arm proposed blocking the beta2-AR/cAMP/MITF drive while the MC1R arm proposed pushing exactly that drive. Nobody noticed either contradiction, because nothing in the protocol required anyone to look at direction. WHERE THE RUN IS NOW CONFIDENT Confident that the redox arm should be retired, not revised. Melatonin's inhibitory evidence spans MT1/MT2-mediated tyrosinase inhibition, epidermal metabolites (6-hydroxymelatonin and others) inhibiting tyrosinase in normal human epidermal melanocytes, p53-TYR suppression of senescence-associated pigmentation, a receptor-independent parallel route, keratinocyte-paracrine suppression, and - decisively for this indication - hair-compartment data: binding localised to the epithelial hair bulb with tyrosinase inhibition in skin organ culture, post-tyrosinase inhibition of melanogenesis in hair follicles in vitro, and inhibition of melanin synthesis plus PROMOTION OF APOPTOSIS in goat hair-follicle melanocytes, which is melanocyte loss, the exact lesion the hypothesis claimed to prevent. Melatonin implants are a long-standing commercial product for driving white winter pelage in fur species. Tetrahydrocurcumin and N-acetylcysteine are documented depigmenting agents (NAC amide is patented for skin whitening). In fairness: the 2022 J Pineal Res review is explicit that melatonin's role in human skin and hair-follicle pigmentation remains unclear, that most inhibitory data are cell-culture-based, and that melatonin has genuine antioxidant, DNA-repair and senescence-protective activity in melanocytes; topical melatonin also has real human growth data in androgenetic alopecia. So the correct statement is not 'melatonin is proven harmful here', it is 'melatonin's documented pigmentary direction is inhibitory, its net effect in intact human follicles is formally unresolved, and that is a reason for a cheap ex vivo direction test, not for a top-ranked candidate in a repigmentation portfolio.' H081 executes this: retire the arm and convert it into a mandatory anti-melanogenic co-exposure exclusion with a 4-week washout applied to every surviving arm. Confident that the beta-blocker lineage is inverted. Beta2-AR signalling is pro-melanogenic in human melanocytes; carvedilol suppresses melanin, tyrosinase, MITF, TRP-1 and TRP-2 and is proposed as a skin-whitening agent; beta-blockers may exacerbate vitiligo; and beta2 agonists are patented to restore pigment. H029 (chronic, part-line) is strictly worse than H039 (event-triggered, concealed zone). H054's rhynchophylline substitution fails twice over, since its source plant extract is a published melanogenesis inhibitor stronger than kojic acid. Confident that H036 should be revised rather than rejected, and confident about why that matters procedurally. Prostaglandin analogues cause poliosis (PMID 15126178: seven patients, bimatoprost among the implicated analogues). The corroborating paper, PMID 15854125, was ALREADY IN THIS RUN'S CORPUS and was never read by any reviewer in five rounds of a prostaglandin arm; the word poliosis appears in zero hypothesis bodies across the entire run. That is a review failure, not a retrieval gap, and it is the most transferable negative result here. The countervailing evidence is genuine and is why the verdict is revise: eyelash darkening in over 50% of bimatoprost users, murine follicular melanogenesis stimulation reported in that same corpus paper, and bimatoprost's deliberate use as a repigmenting agent in vitiligo trials. Net direction still favours darkening. What changes is the stated failure mode: not 'blotchy skin staining' engineered around by a morning wash-off, but 'the treated hairs turn white', which wash-off cannot prevent because the drug works through the follicular reservoir the design preserves. Poliosis must become a consent item and a stop rule. WHERE THE RUN IS STILL GUESSING - INCLUDING ABOUT ITS OWN SURVIVOR The surviving arm is weak in a different way, and the audit is explicit that its weakness is evidence quality, not wrong-direction pharmacology. Every substantive pigment datum for palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 traces to a single supplier-authored source: PMID 30222197 (Int J Cosmet Sci 2018), plus the JID abstract and reseller marketing pages derived from it. That paper must be read precisely, and most of this run misread it: it is in vitro and ex vivo pharmacology. The catalase induction and roughly 30% H2O2 reduction are in human follicle dermal papilla cells, not patients; the 15-volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein expression on plucked hairs after three months of 10 ppm lotion - a target-engagement biomarker, NOT a hair-colour efficacy endpoint - and there was no placebo arm. Human grey-hair endpoint data amount to one uncontrolled case report with concurrent oral supplementation (PMID 37034550). Acetyl hexapeptide-1 is thinner still: its human record is a single confounded case report (PMID 31831925), and its mechanistic support is borrowed from PMID 30222197, a different peptide, correctly cited as class-level support only. There is also a hard efficacy bound the arm already discloses: MC1R is dispensable for acute stress-induced greying in mice (PMID 33382172, in corpus). So the honest position on the run's only survivor is that it is one supplier's unreplicated in vitro/ex vivo package with a biomarker readout and two anecdotes - one tier above nothing, which is where melatonin and tetrahydrocurcumin started, and no more. Also still guessing: the methionine lineage (H052, H066, H082). L-methionine itself is clean and its direct evidence is pro-pigmentary (PMID 19237503, in corpus), but its two principal metabolic fates both point the wrong way - homocysteine chelates copper out of the tyrosinase active site and kills melanocytes via PERK-eIF2alpha-CHOP, and cysteine diverts dopaquinone to pale pheomelanin, with thiols reviewed as clinical skin-lighteners. H066's reduction of the arm to a single gating question (can topical loading raise bulb free methionine above what the bloodstream already supplies?) remains the cleanest example in this run of a hypothesis improving by shrinking, and H082's cancellation of the human protocol plus mandatory eumelanin/pheomelanin and homocysteine contra-readouts is the right next move. Vitamin B12 (H003, H067, H083) is genuinely two-directional: B12 repletion lowers melanin and tyrosinase in the SKIN compartment and resolves deficiency hyperpigmentation, while every hair-specific report has B12 correction reversing white hair. H083 restating the evidence in both directions is correct. VALIDATION-LAYER CAVEATS, ALL STILL TRUE THIS ROUND These are unchanged and they degrade the ranked table itself. (1) Novelty 1.000 is a query artefact on long protocol-string names: 30 of 37 validated hypotheses score 1.000 with co-mention count 0, including every round-4-to-6 evolution, because the 'drug name' being queried is a full protocol description no paper could ever co-mention. Novelty is uninformative for anything above rank 20. (2) The OpenTargets arm ran against MONDO_0005339, androgenetic alopecia - the wrong disease. Every aga_target_overlap in this run is empty, and it would be empty even if the biology were perfect. (3) Evolutions inherit their parent's ChEMBL id, so validation rows describe the wrong molecule. The most damaging instance is the rank-1 hypothesis: H050 is palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 but carries CHEMBL45 and returns targets MTNR1A and MTNR1B - it was validated as melatonin, the agent this round just rejected. (4) 35 source-level validation errors this round, including HTTP 400s from ClinicalTrials.gov on long protocol-string queries and 'no chembl_id available' on essentially every peptide and protocol hypothesis; H064, H078, H079, H080, H081, H082, H083, H051, H040, H066 and H067 all have failed OpenTargets rows. (5) Late-round evolutions are created after the tournament closes and hold inherited Elo with ZERO matches and ZERO reviews. H078, H079, H080, H081, H082 and H083 occupy ranks 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 and 12 on ratings they never contested; 34 of 83 hypotheses have never played a match. The top of the ranked table is systematically the least-scrutinised part of it, and Elo ordering in the top ten should not be read as a preference ordering. (6) The evolution stage is not bound by the candidate universe that generation is bound by, which is how the peptides entered at all - and is a reminder that this run's binding constraint was the generator's universe, not the reviewer's rigour. TOP PICKS AND THE DISCRIMINATING EXPERIMENTS Picks are the contra-clean set only, ordered by what should be done first rather than by Elo. H079 first: it is the correct next experiment, not a trial - independent replication of the palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 melanogenic effect in organ-cultured human anagen follicles by a non-supplier laboratory, plus a Franz-cell/follicular pharmacokinetic study asking whether the peptide reaches the bulb at all. Given that the entire arm rests on one supplier paper, independent replication and target-site delivery are the two questions that carry all the information, and both are cheap relative to a nine-month human protocol. H050 second, as the human protocol to run only if H079 reads out positive: single agent, physically separated clipped squares with true vehicle control, randomised sides, blinded grading, and the within-hair proximal-pigmentation-band endpoint (each shaft carries its own pre-treatment record) - the best design this run produced, and the fix for the H035 reviewer's demonstration that counting incident unpigmented hairs is noise-limited at a few percent per year. H078 is the same molecule with an instrumental shaft-melanin endpoint and a pre-registered non-supplier analysis pipeline and should be merged into H050's protocol rather than run separately; H040 and H064 are the same molecule and should be deduplicated into it. H080 third: the class-replication comparator, acetyl hexapeptide-1 in a parallel cohort under the identical protocol, which is the only design in the run that can distinguish a real MC1R-agonist class effect from an artefact of one supplier's data - the highest-information addition available. H051 fourth as that comparator's standalone form. H037 fifth, not as a therapy but as what the run correctly demoted it to: a mandatory eligibility and stratification gate (B12, ferritin, TSH with anti-TPO) so the topical arms are interpretable at all; it is the one item with no contra-evidence exposure, and H083 is its sharpened successor with selenium and copper deleted and the iron limb declared to have no pigment rationale. H081 is not a candidate but must be adopted as a run-level rule: exclude anti-melanogenic co-exposures and impose a 4-week washout on every surviving arm, since a subject using a melatonin or NAC or tetrahydrocurcumin scalp product would be actively counteracting the intervention. WHAT SIX ROUNDS OF INCREASINGLY SHARP INTERNAL CRITICISM ACTUALLY ACHIEVED Candidly: the criticism got sharper on axes that did not matter and stayed silent on the one that did. Rounds 1-5 produced excellent work on delivery realism, staining of white shafts, contact time, concealed test zones, internal controls, endpoint noise floors, powering, and the conversion of assertions into gating bench experiments. That output is real and transferable, and it is the most valuable thing the run produced. But all of it was engineering applied to agents whose pharmacological sign had never been checked. A perfectly powered, vehicle-controlled, blinded, within-hair-banded nine-month protocol for a documented tyrosinase inhibitor is a well-designed depigmentation study. Increasing review rigour along the dimensions the protocol names does not find errors along the dimension it omits, and no amount of internal criticism substitutes for one missing mandatory question. The single highest-value change in this entire run was a one-line addition to the reviewer harness, made in round 6. Everything else was second-order. This is prioritisation, not proof, and after this audit it is a weaker prioritisation than the preceding three meta-reviews claimed. The defensible reading of 83 hypotheses over six rounds is: one candidate class survives, on a single supplier's unreplicated in vitro/ex vivo evidence with a biomarker rather than a colour endpoint; the correct next step is independent replication and follicular pharmacokinetics (H079) and a class-replication comparator (H080), not a human efficacy trial; and roughly two thirds of the portfolio should be retired on wrong-direction pharmacology. Any downstream reader should treat rounds 1-5 conclusions as unaudited until re-run under the contra-evidence requirement, and should not read Elo rank in the top ten as evidence of scrutiny.

  • Direction-of-effect as the run's real…: does this agent have a documented effect in the OPPOSITE direction, i.e. does it inhibit pigmentation? A contra-evidence requirement was added to the reviewer harness before round 6 and then applied retrospectively to 30 hypotheses. The result inverts the run: 19 came back DOCUMENTED opposite-direction, 7 POSSIBLE, and only 4 CLEAN. The 4 clean ones are H037 (a laboratory panel, which cannot have a pigmentary direction at all) and H040, H050 and H051 - the alpha-MSH biomimetic peptides palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 (Greyverse) and acetyl hexapeptide-1 (Melitane). Everything else in the portfolio either is, or plausibly metabolises into, a melanogenesis inhibitor. This theme is not a caveat attached to the findings; it is the finding.
  • The redox arm, which led…: MT1/MT2-receptor-mediated inhibition of tyrosinase and melanocyte proliferation; the metabolite 6-hydroxymelatonin and others produced in human epidermis inhibiting tyrosinase in normal human epidermal melanocytes from 1e-9 M; suppression of senescence-associated pigmentation through the p53-TYR pathway; a receptor-independent route that luzindole and 4-P-PDOT do not block; keratinocyte-paracrine suppression of MITF/TYR/TYRP1/DCT. Critically it is not only a skin finding: melatonin binding sites were localised to the epithelial hair bulb with tyrosinase inhibition in skin organ culture, post-tyrosinase inhibition of melanogenesis was shown in hair follicles in vitro, melatonin inhibits melanin synthesis and PROMOTES APOPTOSIS in goat hair-follicle melanocytes, and melatonin implants are a decades-old commercial veterinary product used to drive white winter pelage in fur species. Tetrahydrocurcumin is sold as a skin-whitening active and has a mechanism paper devoted to optimising the same lipophilic topical delivery H026 specifies; N-acetylcysteine lowers melanin in human melanocytic cells via FoxO3a and supplies the cysteine that diverts eumelanin to pale pheomelanin, and NAC amide is patented for skin whitening. In fairness, and this is the honest residue: the 2022 J Pineal Res review states the human skin and hair-follicle role of melatonin remains unclear, most inhibitory data are cell-culture-based, melatonin does have genuine antioxidant, senescence-protective and cytoprotective activity in melanocytes, and topical melatonin has real growth data in androgenetic alopecia. That is a case for an ex vivo direction test, not for a top-ranked repigmentation candidate. H081 (retire the arm, convert it into an anti-melanogenic co-exposure exclusion and 4-week washout for the surviving arms) is the correct disposition.
  • The stress-axis arm is inverted…: its source plant extract is published as a melanogenesis inhibitor stronger than kojic acid. No hypothesis in five rounds noticed that the run was simultaneously proposing to press and to release the same pedal.
  • The prostaglandin arm and a…: seven glaucoma patients on topical PGF2alpha analogues developed bilateral poliosis (PMID 15126178, external to this corpus), with bimatoprost - H036's exact molecule - among the implicated agents. The damning detail is that the corroborating paper was already inside this run's own 542-record corpus: PMID 15854125 (Sasaki, Hozumi, Kondo, Exp Dermatol 2005) states PGF2alpha analogues cause hypertrichosis and hyperpigmentation and OCCASIONALLY POLIOSIS, and reports a case of white hair emerging. The word poliosis appears in ZERO hypothesis bodies across all six rounds despite the prostaglandin arm running for five of them. That is a review failure, not a retrieval gap - the evidence was retrieved and nobody read it. The countervailing evidence is real and is why H036 is revise and not reject: eyelash darkening occurs in over 50% of bimatoprost users, the same corpus paper PMID 15854125 shows PGF2alpha and its analogues stimulating murine follicular melanogenesis in telogen and anagen, and bimatoprost is used deliberately as a repigmenting agent in vitiligo trials. Net weight still favours darkening. But the failure mode was misstated for five rounds as 'blotchy skin staining', which the design engineers around with a morning wash-off; the actual failure mode is that the treated hairs go white, and wash-off does nothing about it because the drug acts through the follicular reservoir the design deliberately preserves.
  • The only surviving molecular candidates…: exhaustive search of PubMed, cosmetic patents, INCI and supplier positioning found no depigmenting, whitening or leukotrichia signal, no anti-melanogenic metabolite (palmitic acid plus short peptides), and commercial positioning that runs the same way as the claim. Two apparent lightening leads for acetyl hexapeptide-1 were chased to their sources and both collapsed (retailer marketing copy; a patent whose 'lightening' claim is a mistranslation applied to wrinkles). This is the run's most uncomfortable structural finding: this class could not have been proposed in rounds 1-3, because the candidate universe was ChEMBL drugs plus supplements until round 4. The one arm that survived contact with reality entered the run only after the universe was widened, and it entered near the bottom, with the plain-molecule form H040 sitting at baseline 1500 Elo on a single review. The generator's candidate universe, not the reviewer, was the binding constraint on this run's quality.
  • Protocol and design engineering was…: oral to topical, colourless tetrahydrocurcumin to avoid staining white shafts, an internal hemi-scalp control (H035), contact-time limitation and wash-off (H036), a concealed non-periocular test zone (H051), physically separated clipped squares with a true vehicle control and a within-hair proximal-pigmentation-band endpoint where each shaft is its own pre-treatment record (H050, inherited by H052 and H064), and the round-5 move from protocol to prerequisite - H065 and H066 both converting their central unproven assertion into a short bench experiment that gates the human trial. Round 6's evolutions (H078-H083) are the clearest expression of this: facing a surviving arm whose entire evidence base is one supplier-authored paper, they did NOT add new agents. H078 adds an instrumental shaft-melanin endpoint and a non-supplier pre-registered analysis pipeline; H079 cancels the human trial in favour of independent ex vivo replication plus follicular pharmacokinetics; H080 repurposes the duplicate Melitane arm into a pre-specified class-replication comparator to test whether MC1R-agonist pigment effects reproduce across molecules or are an artefact of one supplier's data; H082 cancels the methionine human protocol and adds a mandatory eumelanin/pheomelanin and homocysteine contra-readout; H083 restates the B12 evidence in both directions. Attacking independent replication and measurement rather than adding candidates is the correct response to this run's actual state.

Top picks: H079, H050, H080, H051, H037

Candidate details

1. palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 (Greyverse) 2% leave-on scalp solution as a single-agent replacement for the melatonin + tetrahydrocurcumin redox pair, tested in vehicle-controlled clipped mid-scalp squares with a proximal-shaft repigmentation-band endpoint (H050) Novel candidate — TOP PICK

ChEMBL: CHEMBL45 · Elo 1584 · confidence 3/5 · route: topical (leave-on solution, vehicle-controlled clipped test squares) · origin: evolution (round 4) · theme: Direction-of-effect as the run's real…

Mechanism
The strategic target of both parents is kept — reduce the follicular peroxide burden and restore the follicle's own catalase capacity, since grey follicles carry millimolar H2O2 with near-absent catalase and methionine-sulfoxide reductase, which sulfoxidises Met374 in the tyrosinase active site and progressively disables melanogenesis. What changes is the molecule carrying that strategy. Melatonin scavenges stoichiometrically and tetrahydrocurcumin's catalase induction is inferred from non-follicular tissue; neither has any measurement in a hair follicle. Palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 is an alpha-MSH biomimetic that engages MC1R and, in the same published work, raised catalase enzymatic activity and lowered intracellular H2O2 by about 30% in human follicle dermal papilla cells, and altered catalase, TRP-1, TRP-2, Melan-A, ASIP and MC1-R expression in ex vivo microdissected human hairs. That is the same two-level attack the parents wanted (lower peroxide plus restore enzymatic clearance) but delivered by one lipid-conjugated peptide with follicle-derived-cell data, plus an orthogonal pro-melanogenic MC1R-cAMP-MITF push that the antioxidant pair never had. One agent, so a null or positive result is attributable.
Evidence
PMID 19237503 (Wood et al.) is the corpus-verified anchor for the lesion: FT-Raman evidence of millimolar H2O2 in grey shafts, near-absent catalase and MSRA/B protein across the whole grey follicle, and Met-S=O at tyrosinase Met374 limiting enzyme function. PMID 33598985 shows H2O2 and noradrenaline driving ectopic bulge differentiation in ex vivo human follicles; PMID 36853923 frames MelSC loss as the central driver. PMID 30222197 is the intervention evidence and must be read precisely: it is an in vitro and ex vivo pharmacology paper. The catalase-induction and 30% H2O2-reduction data come from human follicle dermal papilla cells, not from patients; the 15-volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein expression on plucked hairs after three months of daily 10 ppm lotion, which is a target-engagement biomarker, NOT a hair-colour efficacy endpoint, and there was no placebo arm. Human grey-hair-endpoint data amount to a single uncontrolled case report of Greyverse 2% with concurrent oral biotin/pantothenate supplementation (PMID 37034550) - anecdote grade and confounded. So intervention evidence is no longer literally zero as it was for melatonin and tetrahydrocurcumin, but it is supplier-developed, uncontrolled, and one tier above nothing. The design objections raised against H035 are answered concretely rather than by adding a control label: two clipped 2 cm squares in the mid-parietal scalp separated by at least 5 cm of untreated scalp to blunt sebum-borne crossover, active on one and identical vehicle on the other with side randomised, application by separate labelled applicators to equalise massage, and the primary endpoint changed from counting incident unpigmented hairs (which the reviewer correctly showed is noise-limited at a few percent per year) to per-hair proximal pigmentation banding: at 6 and 9 months, 50 hairs are cut from each square and scored blind for a pigmented proximal segment beneath an unpigmented distal shaft. Each hair is its own before/after control, which removes zone-registration error and the problem that hairs emerging early were committed before treatment started. (refs: PMID 19237503, PMID 30222197, PMID 37034550, PMID 33598985, PMID 36853923)
Safety
Cosmetic-grade leave-on peptide sold OTC as a scalp solution; systemic exposure at 10 ppm to 2% topical scale is negligible and no adverse-effect signal is described in the published work. Realistic worst case is local irritation or contact sensitisation to the peptide or vehicle preservatives - patch-test the forearm for three days first. MC1R agonism is pigment-pathway signalling on the scalp only; unlike systemic melanocortin analogues there is no meaningful risk of generalised skin darkening or nausea at these doses, though users with many atypical naevi should have a baseline skin check on general principle. No prescriber, no drug interactions, satisfies the run's OTC constraint. Explicit stop rule: if blinded band scoring shows no divergence between active and vehicle squares at 9 months, stop - do not extend and do not stack further actives on top.
Novelty rationale
This is a compound substitution plus an endpoint substitution, not a rewording. The parents' redox arm is retired because both of its agents had zero follicular measurement, and is replaced by the only OTC active in the universe with published catalase-induction and H2O2-reduction data in follicle-derived cells plus a human grey-hair case report - which is exactly the 'intervention evidence is empty' hole both reviewers named. The two-agent stack is collapsed to one agent, fixing the attribution problem the H035 reviewer raised. The hemi-scalp part-line design, which the same reviewer showed is contaminated by construction on a sebum-continuous scalp, is replaced by physically separated clipped squares with a true vehicle control and a blinded grader. Most importantly the endpoint moves from an underpowered incidence count to within-hair proximal banding, where each shaft carries its own pre-treatment record. It also corrects the widespread misreading of PMID 30222197 as a clinical efficacy trial, which matters because that misreading is what makes this active look stronger than it is.
Reviews
  • plausibility 7/10, evidence 5/10, safety 8/10, novelty 3/10 — verdict revise. The strongest entry in this batch, and the only one whose citations I could not break. The reading of PMID 30222197 is now correct and explicit: catalase induction and the ~30% H2O2 drop are in HFDPC (dermal papilla, not melanocytes and not bulge), the ex vivo arm is microdissected hair expression, and the 15-volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein on plucked hairs with no placebo - a target-engagement biomarker, not efficacy. Keeping that correction in the text is the single most valuable thing here, because the paper's own title says 'Efficacy ... in hair pigmentation' and everyone downstream reads it as a trial. Remaining weak links, in order. (1) Novelty is close to zero at the compound level. Greyverse/palmitoyl tetrapeptide is already named in the dossier's cosmetic-actives section as a marketed alpha-MSH-mimetic anti-grey product; proposing it is proposing the incumbent. What is actually new is the protocol, and the hypothesis should be scored and sold as a trial-design contribution, not as repurposing. (2) The human anchor, PMID 37034550, is worse than 'anecdote grade and confounded' - it is biologically strained. It claims >90% conversion of grey to black scalp hair at 5 months in a 25-year-old on concurrent oral biotin/pantothenate. At ~1 cm/month, five months of new anagen cannot repigment shafts that are 15-30 cm long, so either the assessment is of proximal scalp appearance only or the result is unreliable; cite it as a red flag, not as support. (3) Mechanistic mismatch left unaddressed: the redox lesion in PMID 19237503 is catalase/MSRA-B loss across the whole follicle including the shaft, but the catalase-induction datum is in dermal papilla fibroblasts, which are neither the melanocyte nor the shaft. The claim that this is 'the same two-level attack' therefore rests on cross-compartment extrapolation the hypothesis criticises elsewhere. (4) The design is good but underspecified: no sample size, no number of subjects, no stated grader-reliability check, and no pre-registered effect size for the band score. With a 50-hair-per-square sample and a subjective band call, a small real effect is indistinguishable from grader drift. (5) Housekeeping: chembl_id CHEMBL45 is not palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 - a lipopeptide of that class has no such early small-molecule ChEMBL entry - so the identifier is wrong and should be null rather than a wrong ID that will propagate. (6) PMID 33382172 (MC1R dispensable for acute stress-induced greying in mice) belongs in this hypothesis as a scope limit, as it is in H051: an MC1R-driven agent should not be expected to touch the stress arm. Revise: fix the ChEMBL ID, downgrade PMID 37034550 explicitly, add power/n and the MC1R scope caveat, and reframe novelty as protocol rather than molecule.

2. topical L-methionine, restructured as a single gating pre-experiment - can topical loading raise free methionine in plucked anagen bulbs above the plasma-supplied baseline at all - with the 9-month human protocol contingent on that answer (H066) Novel candidate —

ChEMBL: n/a · Elo 1580 · confidence 2/5 · route: topical (occluded lotion on clipped squares, as a two-week plucked-bulb quantitation experiment before any efficacy protocol) · origin: evolution (round 5) · theme: Protocol and design engineering was…

Mechanism
The parent's reviewer landed two blows that cannot be argued away, and this version concedes both rather than defending them. First, the parent's central chemical claim - that sacrificial protection 'does not require out-competing the flux, only protecting one active site within it' - is withdrawn as indefensible. Free methionine and Met374 compete for the same oxidant through the same slow second-order reaction, so protection is set entirely by the concentration ratio of free methionine to protein-bound methionine at the site; this is a competition argument and it is now stated as one, with the corollary that meaningful protection needs sustained high free methionine in the bulb melanocyte against a continuously regenerated peroxide pool, with no catalytic turnover to help. Second, and decisively, the reviewer asked why endogenous methionine fails: the follicle is perfused with plasma methionine and every patient eats it daily, yet they grey. This version accepts that this is the question the hypothesis lives or dies on, and refuses to run a nine-month human protocol before answering it. So the hypothesis is restructured into its gating pre-experiment. Clipped scalp squares receive 10% w/v L-methionine in a water/propylene-glycol vehicle under a 6-8 hour overnight occlusive cap, or vehicle alone, for two weeks; anagen bulbs are then plucked from both squares and free methionine is quantified by LC-MS/MS against the contralateral vehicle bulbs and against normal plasma concentration as the reference the mechanism must beat. The prediction is explicit and falsifying: if topical loading cannot raise bulb free methionine substantially above the level the bloodstream already supplies, the sacrificial mechanism is dead on arrival regardless of formulation cleverness, because the patient's own circulation has already run the experiment for a lifetime and lost. That is a cheap, fast, unambiguous kill test for an idea that would otherwise consume a year.
Evidence
PMID 19237503 remains the entire basis and is corpus-verified: in vivo FT-Raman millimolar H2O2 in grey shafts, almost absent catalase and methionine sulfoxide reductase A and B protein, functional loss of Met-S=O repair throughout the grey follicle including the shaft, Met374 sulfoxidation limiting tyrosinase kinetics by enzyme kinetics and computer simulation, and the single clause the whole proposal rests on - that under in vitro conditions Met oxidation can be prevented by L-methionine. That clause is now weighted correctly: it is a cuvette result at whatever ratio the cuvette contained, not evidence about a cell. PMID 32549695 is demoted to context and explicitly not cited as support for this molecule, since it measures serum MDA, glutathione and SOD - systemic redox, not follicular. PMID 32965076 and PMID 38099887 sharpen the compartment question the parent tried to duck by saying the deficit spans the whole follicle: human greying begins as declining melanogenesis with reduced tyrosinase activity in the anagen bulb, so the target is a specific enzyme inside the melanosomal pathway of a bulb melanocyte, and the lesion being everywhere does not put the drug everywhere. The uptake objection is also conceded in the mechanism rather than hidden: methionine entering a cell is handled by amino-acid transporters and consumed by protein synthesis, so free intracellular methionine is buffered, which is one more reason the measurement must come first. PMID 36853923 frames the loss process. Honest ceiling, unchanged and now unhedged: no one has applied methionine to a human scalp for pigmentation, no follicular concentration measurement exists anywhere, and there is no greying endpoint of any grade. This is the least-supported active in the surviving set and it is proposed only because its failure mode is cheap and unambiguous. (refs: PMID 19237503, PMID 32965076, PMID 38099887, PMID 36853923, PMID 32549695)
Safety
L-methionine is an essential dietary amino acid sold as food-grade powder for pennies per gram; topical exposure carries no systemic risk, and the homocysteine concerns attached to methionine are about chronic high oral loading, which is not proposed. The pre-experiment shortens exposure from nine months to two weeks, which proportionately shrinks the only real risks: glycol-vehicle irritation, and maceration or folliculitis under occlusion. Occlusion is capped at one overnight period per application, the cap is washed or discarded between uses, and use stops on any pustules or persistent erythema. Three-day forearm patch test first. Anyone with active scalp dermatitis does not occlude. Plucking 15-20 anagen bulbs per square is the only invasive step. No prescriber, no interactions.
Novelty rationale
The parent was revised-with-conditions and this executes both conditions literally rather than partially. The indefensible claim - that the mechanism escapes having to out-compete the peroxide flux - is deleted and replaced with an explicit concentration-ratio competition argument that states what would have to be true for the idea to work. And the reviewer's demanded penetration/quantitation gate is not appended as a suggested extra step; it becomes the whole hypothesis, with the nine-month human protocol demoted to a contingency that only exists if the two-week assay reports a positive. The change of shape matters more than the change of wording: the parent proposed an untested molecule on an untested route for an untested mechanism and hoped a long trial would sort it out, whereas this version identifies the single measurement that discriminates between all the failure modes at once - if bulb free methionine cannot exceed what plasma already delivers, no formulation will rescue it and the branch is closed honestly. Answering the endogenous-methionine paradox directly, rather than not attempting it as the parent did, is the substantive intellectual change.
Reviews
  • plausibility 5/10, evidence 4/10, safety 8/10, novelty 3/10 — verdict revise. Restructuring into a two-week kill test is the right instinct and I credit it, but the kill test as specified cannot deliver the verdict it promises, and that is the weakest link. Three flaws. (1) Assay contamination: 10% w/v L-methionine is applied under overnight occlusion to the same square from which anagen bulbs are then plucked. Plucked bulbs drag the outer root sheath and infundibular contents with them; a lotion sitting in the infundibulum at 100 mg/mL will dwarf any intracellular signal by orders of magnitude. There is no wash-out interval, no surfactant strip, no isotopically labelled tracer, and no infundibular-contamination control, so a 'positive' is uninterpretable as target engagement. (2) The null is equally uninterpretable, and the hypothesis says so itself without drawing the consequence: it concedes free intracellular methionine is buffered by transporters and consumed by protein synthesis, so the drug can reach the melanocyte and still show no rise in free pool. A test whose positive is confounded and whose negative is non-informative is not a kill test. (3) The comparator is wrong. 'Normal plasma concentration' is the benchmark chosen because it makes the endogenous-methionine paradox tractable, but the mechanism is about the ratio of free Met to protein-bound Met at Met374 inside the melanosome, not about whole-bulb free Met versus plasma. Beating plasma in a bulb homogenate says nothing about the melanosomal ratio, which is the only number the competition argument actually turns on. Beyond the assay: PMID 19237503 is cited accurately, and I confirm the load-bearing clause verbatim ('under in vitro conditions, Met oxidation can be prevented by L-methionine'), but the hypothesis is right that this is a cuvette at an unstated ratio - it is a single sentence in a 2009 paper with no concentration reported, which is thin ground for a programme. The demotion of PMID 32549695 to context is correct and well reasoned. Novelty is overstated: L-methionine for canities on exactly this MSRA/Met374 logic is already an explicit published suggestion in the premature-greying review literature, so this is a well-worn proposal, not a new repurposing idea.
    contra-evidence (possible): Searches run outside the bundle: (a) 'L-methionine tyrosinase inhibition melanin skin whitening cosmetic' - no evidence that methionine itself is sold or used as a whitening active; the commercial tyrosinase-inhibitor space is hydroquinone/arbutin/kojic/azelaic/tranexamic/ascorbic and thiols, not methionine. Methionine is sold as a food-grade amino acid and as a urinary acidifier, not as a cosmetic depigmentant. (b) 'homocysteine premature hair greying canities methionine loading hyperhomocysteinemia vitiligo' - this is where opposite-direction evidence lives. Methionine is the direct metabolic precursor of homocysteine, and homocysteine inhibits tyrosinase and releases ROS that inhibit melanin synthesis and damage melanocytes; raised homocysteine is documented in vitiligo case-control work and is discussed as a mechanism shared with premature greying. Loading methionine is the standard clinical way to RAISE homocysteine. (c) 'methionine restriction cysteine pheomelanin eumelanin switch' - methionine feeds cysteine via transsulfuration; cysteine is the switch that diverts dopaquinone to cysteinyl-DOPA and yellow-red pheomelanin, and cysteine DEPRIVATION is published as promoting eumelanogenesis and visible pigmentation in human melanoma cells (PMID 8875952), with thiol compounds reviewed as clinical skin-lightening agents. So the two principal metabolic fates of a methionine load - homocysteine and cysteine - both have published depigmenting/eumelanin-suppressing effects, pointing opposite to the claim. What I did NOT find: any report of topical or oral methionine causing hair or skin lightening in vivo, and no whitening product built on it. Verdict therefore 'possible', not 'documented': the parent molecule is clean, its metabolites are not.

3. palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 (Greyverse) 2% leave-on solution as the run's single MC1R arm, retargeted at the anagen hair bulb pigmentary unit rather than the bulge, with a powered vehicle-controlled paired-square protocol and a trichoscopic staging gate (H064) Novel candidate —

ChEMBL: n/a · Elo 1576 · confidence 3/5 · route: topical (leave-on solution, vehicle-controlled paired clipped squares) · origin: evolution (round 5) · theme: The only surviving molecular candidates…

Mechanism
The two parents were the same idea bought from two suppliers: both are OTC alpha-MSH biomimetics reaching MITF and tyrosinase through MC1R-cAMP. The H051 reviewer's instruction was explicit - run one MC1R arm, and run the one with follicle-derived-cell data - so acetyl hexapeptide-1 is deleted rather than run in parallel, and with it the cross-molecule class extrapolation from a palmitoylated tetrapeptide to an acetylated hexapeptide that this run has penalised elsewhere. What is new beyond the merge is where the drug is aimed. Both parents inherited the run-wide habit of framing every candidate as MelSC (bulge) protection, but the human-tissue literature says the opposite about the treatable stage: greying invariably begins as a decline of melanogenesis in the anagen hair bulb - reduced tyrosinase activity, defective melanosome transfer to precortical matrix keratinocytes, apoptosis of pigmentary-unit melanocytes - and only becomes largely irreversible later, once bulge melanocyte stem cells are also depleted. That relocates the therapeutic window precisely where an MC1R agonist can act, because MC1R-cAMP-MITF drives melanogenic output in differentiated bulb melanocytes, not stem-cell maintenance in the bulge. It also explains why this agent should be expected to do nothing for the stress arm of the disease, where MC1R is dispensable, and nothing at all in a follicle that has already crossed into the stem-cell-depleted stage. The hypothesis is therefore narrowed to one claim: in follicles still in the reversible, output-deficient stage, MC1R agonism raises pigment output per anagen, visible as a pigmented proximal band beneath an unpigmented distal shaft.
Evidence
PMID 32965076 and PMID 38099887 are the load-bearing new citations and they are what make this a different hypothesis rather than a merge: both state that human greying begins with declining melanogenesis in the anagen bulb (reduced tyrosinase activity, defective melanosome transfer, HFPU melanocyte apoptosis), that bulge stem-cell depletion is a later event which is what makes greying irreversible, and that early anagen is the phase most conducive to melanocytic reintegration. PMID 30222197 remains the intervention evidence and the correct reading is carried forward unchanged, because it is the single most valuable line in the parent: its title says 'Efficacy ... in hair pigmentation' but it is in vitro and ex vivo pharmacology - catalase induction and the ~30% H2O2 fall are in human follicle dermal papilla cells, which are neither melanocytes nor bulge, and the 15-volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein on plucked hairs after three months with no placebo, i.e. target engagement, not hair colour. The cross-compartment problem the H050 reviewer named is not argued away: the dermal-papilla catalase datum is retained only as evidence the peptide is bioactive in follicle-derived cells, and the pigment claim now rests on the melanogenic data (melanin production up, MC1-R, TRP-1 and Melan-A up, ASIP down, in vitro and in ex vivo microdissected human hairs), not on a redox mechanism borrowed from fibroblasts. PMID 37034550 is downgraded from support to red flag exactly as instructed: >90% grey-to-black conversion at five months in one 25-year-old on concurrent oral biotin and pantothenate is not compatible with ~1 cm/month growth on 15-30 cm shafts, so it is either a proximal-scalp impression or unreliable, and it is cited as a reason for caution about supplier-adjacent case reports rather than as human efficacy. PMID 19237503 supplies the underlying lesion (millimolar H2O2, absent catalase and MSRA/B, Met374 sulfoxidation limiting tyrosinase kinetics). PMID 33382172 bounds the scope: MC1R is dispensable for acute stress-induced greying in mice, so this arm should not be expected to touch the stress axis. PMID 36853923 supplies the exclusion rule for fully white zones. The design gaps the H050 reviewer listed are closed with numbers rather than adjectives: 24 subjects with early-to-mid canities, two clipped 2 cm squares at least 5 cm apart in the mid-parietal/vertex region - which is where premature greying actually is (vertex 42.5% in PMID 36386090; temporal 35.2% and frontal 18.3% in PMID 28584374) and which is concealable under surrounding hair, replacing H051's retroauricular site that may contain no grey at all - side randomised, identical vehicle on the control square, separate labelled applicators, 50 hairs cut per square at 6 and 9 months, scored blind for a pigmented proximal segment, with 20% of hairs re-scored by a second blinded grader and a pre-declared kappa of at least 0.8 before any efficacy read. Pre-registered minimum interesting effect: a 15 percentage-point absolute difference in the proportion of banded hairs between active and vehicle squares. Enrolment gate inherited from H051: 20x trichoscopy, and a square qualifies only if it is salt-and-pepper with a majority of shafts still pigmented, which under the new staging argument is not merely a power trick but the operational definition of the reversible stage. (refs: PMID 30222197, PMID 32965076, PMID 38099887, PMID 19237503, PMID 37034550, PMID 33382172, PMID 36853923, PMID 36386090, PMID 28584374)
Safety
Cosmetic-grade OTC leave-on peptide; no prescriber, no drug interactions, negligible systemic absorption of a lipid-conjugated tetrapeptide at 2% on two 2 cm squares. Realistic worst case is local irritation or contact sensitisation to the peptide or the vehicle preservatives, so a three-day forearm patch test comes first. As a melanogenesis stimulant it can in principle darken the treated skin as well as the hair; squares are sited under overlying hair so any such change is concealed, and the stop rule is retained - discontinue on visible perifollicular darkening, blotching or persistent erythema. Users with many atypical naevi should have a baseline skin check on general principle. Explicit stop rule for the programme: if blinded banding shows less than the pre-registered 15-point difference at 9 months, stop; do not extend, do not stack further actives, and do not re-analyse subgroups.
Novelty rationale
Three material changes, none of them rewording. (1) The duplicate MC1R arm is eliminated: acetyl hexapeptide-1 is deleted along with its cross-molecule evidence transfer and its single confounded 14-year-old case report, which the H051 reviewer identified as the thinnest human anchor in the batch, and H051's two genuinely useful contributions - the trichoscopic staging gate and the concealment requirement - are absorbed into the surviving arm and reconciled with where premature canities actually occurs, which the retroauricular site was not. (2) The mechanistic target is moved from bulge MelSC protection to anagen-bulb melanogenic output on the strength of PMID 32965076 and PMID 38099887, neither of which either parent cited. This is not cosmetic: it converts the enrolment criterion from a power convenience into a staging criterion, it explains why the agent should fail in white zones and in the stress arm, and it removes the parents' reliance on a redox story anchored in dermal papilla fibroblasts by putting the pigment claim on the melanogenic data instead. (3) The housekeeping the reviewer demanded is done: the wrong ChEMBL ID (CHEMBL45, which is melatonin, not a lipopeptide) is set to null rather than propagated; PMID 37034550 is reclassified as a red flag with the growth-rate arithmetic stated; and n, grader-reliability and a pre-registered effect size replace 'blinded scoring' as a phrase. Novelty is claimed where it exists - staging-gated protocol and target relocation - and explicitly not at the compound level, since Greyverse is the marketed incumbent.
Reviews
  • plausibility 7/10, evidence 5/10, safety 7/10, novelty 3/10 — verdict revise. The strongest arm in this batch, and the citation handling is unusually honest - the reclassification of PMID 37034550 as a red flag on growth-rate arithmetic is correct (>90% conversion of 15-30 cm shafts in five months at ~1 cm/month is arithmetically impossible), the scope limit from PMID 33382172 is faithfully read (MC1R dispensable for acute stress greying in mice), and the ChEMBL correction is real. The relocation of the target from bulge to anagen bulb is genuinely supported by PMID 32965076 and PMID 38099887 and is the best mechanistic move made anywhere in this round. Weakest links: (1) All melanogenic evidence traces to a single supplier-authored paper. PMID 30222197 is Almeida Scalvino et al. in Int J Cosmet Sci, authored from the ingredient house that sells Greyverse, and I could find no independent replication - the only other human item is the confounded single case report the hypothesis itself red-flags. The pigment claim rests on one non-independent source with no placebo-controlled colour endpoint anywhere in the literature, and the in vitro effect sizes are modest (melanin +19% at 1e-7 M, +39% in melanocytes, +35% ex vivo bulb pigmentation after 7 days). (2) The 15-hair volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein on plucked hairs with no placebo - the hypothesis grades this correctly as target engagement, but that leaves the human evidence for colour at literally zero. (3) The power claim is asserted, not shown. 'Powered' appears in the title but there is no power calculation, and the analysis unit is ambiguous: 50 hairs per square from 24 subjects is 1200 hairs but 24 independent units, and hairs within a subject are strongly clustered. Without a stated ICC and a subject-level or mixed-model analysis, a 15-point difference in the proportion of banded hairs across 24 subjects is very likely underpowered, and pseudoreplicating at the hair level would manufacture significance. Fix this before anything else. (4) The staging gate ('salt-and-pepper, majority of shafts still pigmented') has no operational threshold - 'majority' is not a criterion two graders can apply reproducibly, despite the admirable kappa >= 0.8 requirement applied downstream to banding.
    contra-evidence (possible): Searches run outside the bundle: (a) 'alpha-MSH MC1R agonist afamelanotide adverse effects hypopigmentation depigmentation melanoma risk' - MC1R agonism is overwhelmingly documented as PRO-pigmentary (afamelanotide causes hyperpigmentation in ~1/3 of patients; increases eumelanin), which is the claimed direction, not the opposite. Two caveats found: a published case of depigmented facial and neck patches following melanotan use (Clin Exp Dermatol) in a patient with atopic dermatitis and alopecia areata - confounded by atopy/autoimmunity and by a systemic, illicit, non-identical peptide; and reports of eruptive/atypical naevi and melanoma with illicitly obtained melanotan, with causality unclear. Neither is transferable to a 2% topical lipopeptide on two 2 cm squares, but the naevus signal justifies the baseline skin check the hypothesis already requires. (b) 'palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 Greyverse independent clinical trial evidence' - what this search actually refuted is the independence of the evidence base, not its direction: every substantive datum (JID abstract 1384, Int J Cosmet Sci 2018, and the marketing pages at multiple resellers) traces to the same supplier study. The compound is sold commercially precisely for anti-grey hair, so commercial use is aligned with the claim, not against it - no whitening or depigmenting product uses it. (c) Corpus check: no record in the 542-record corpus reports depigmentation from an MC1R agonist. Conclusion: no documented opposite-direction effect for this molecule; a weak, confounded systemic-analogue depigmentation case report keeps this at 'possible' rather than 'none_found'.

4. acetyl hexapeptide-1 (Melitane) 5% leave-on lotion, repurposed from a duplicate MC1R arm into a pre-specified class-replication comparator run in a parallel cohort under the identical vehicle-controlled protocol, to test whether MC1R-agonist pigment effects are reproducible across molecules or an artefact of one supplier's data (H080) Novel candidate — TOP PICK

ChEMBL: n/a · Elo 1571 · confidence 2/5 · route: topical (leave-on lotion, vehicle-controlled paired clipped squares, parallel cohort to the palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 arm) · origin: evolution (round 6) · theme: The only surviving molecular candidates…

Mechanism
The H051 reviewer's instruction was to run one MC1R arm and delete the other, and H064 complied by deleting this peptide. That was right on the assumption that the two molecules are interchangeable tests of the same idea - but it is wrong given what the contra-audit and the round-6 reviews then established: the decisive weakness of the surviving arm is not its mechanism, it is that all of its pigment evidence descends from a single supplier-authored paper on a single molecule with no independent replication anywhere. Under that condition a second, chemically distinct MC1R biomimetic is not a duplicate, it is the control for the failure mode. Acetyl hexapeptide-1 (Ac-Nle-Ala-His-D-Phe-Arg-Trp-NH2) is an alpha-MSH biomimetic built on the canonical His-D-Phe-Arg-Trp melanocortin pharmacophore and sold by a different manufacturer, whereas palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 is a lipid-anchored tetrapeptide; they share the receptor and the cAMP-MITF-tyrosinase axis but not the chemistry, the formulation or the commercial sponsor. If a real MC1R-mediated pigment effect exists in human anagen bulbs, both should move the endpoint; if only the supplier's own molecule does, the effect is a property of the source rather than of the receptor. So the design is a three-arm parallel-group study in separate subjects at a separate site: cohort 1 receives palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 2% versus its vehicle on paired squares, cohort 2 receives acetyl hexapeptide-1 5% versus the identical vehicle on paired squares, everything else - siting, staging gate, instrumented endpoint, blinding, subject-level model - held constant. Running it in a parallel cohort rather than as a third square on the same scalp is deliberate: two actives on one head reintroduces sebum-borne crossover and destroys attribution, which is the objection that killed the stacked designs earlier in this run.
Evidence
The molecule-specific human evidence for acetyl hexapeptide-1 is one uncontrolled case report - PMID 31831925, a 14-year-old given Melitane 5% together with oral hair supplements, reporting improvement in hair colour with no objective measure, no control and an obvious co-intervention. That is anecdote grade and is the thinnest human anchor in the top set, and it is labelled as such here rather than leaned on. The cross-molecule evidence transfer the H051 reviewer struck out is not restored: PMID 30222197 studies palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20, a different molecule, and in this version it is not cited as support for acetyl hexapeptide-1 at all - it is cited as the claim under test, which is the whole point of running the second molecule. Receptor-level grounding is PMID 33320376 for the alpha-MSH to MC1R to cAMP to MITF architecture; PMID 25219681 for the clinical relevance of MC1R polymorphism, which is now a pre-specified covariate in both cohorts because a loss-of-function receptor predicts non-response to both agents and would produce a spurious 'class failure'; PMID 32965076 and PMID 38099887 for the anagen-bulb target and the reversible stage; PMID 31316609 and PMID 34700322 for the transcriptional pigment deficit. Two constraints from the parent are kept because they are correct: PMID 36853923 excludes uniformly white zones, where non-response is guaranteed by the absence of a target and would be misread as drug failure, and PMID 33382172 bounds both arms away from the stress axis, MC1R being dispensable for acute stress-induced greying in mice. The parent's two design regressions are repaired: a vehicle-treated control square and blinded, instrument-read scoring are added (H051 had neither, judging a single treated patch), and the test site moves from the retroauricular/mid-occipital zone to the mid-parietal/vertex region where premature canities actually occurs and where the 15-50% white-shaft staging gate can plausibly be met (vertex 42.5% in PMID 36386090; temporal 35.2%, frontal 18.3% in PMID 28584374) while still being concealed under surrounding hair. Pre-registered interpretation, declared before unblinding: concordant positive in both cohorts supports a class effect; positive only for the supplier's own molecule is read as evidence against the class claim and against the supplier dataset, not as a win for that molecule; concordant null retires the MC1R arm entirely. (refs: PMID 31831925, PMID 30222197, PMID 33320376, PMID 25219681, PMID 32965076, PMID 38099887, PMID 31316609, PMID 34700322, PMID 36853923, PMID 33382172, PMID 36386090, PMID 28584374)
Safety
Cosmetic-grade OTC peptide lotion, no prescriber, no cost barrier, negligible systemic absorption of a modified hexapeptide. The round-6 contra-evidence audit examined this molecule directly - supplier and INCI positioning, cosmetic patents including two apparent lightening leads that were fetched in full and collapsed (a CN patent claiming only anti-wrinkle, antibacterial and moisturising effects, and an unrelated whitening-peptide EP filing), and PubMed - and found no depigmenting, tyrosinase-inhibiting or poliosis report anywhere; commercial positioning is the opposite of a whitener, the active being sold as a pigment-boosting/self-tanning ingredient. It also confirmed no naming collision with the acetyl pentapeptide tyrosinase inhibitors or with acetyl hexapeptide-8/Argireline. So, like its sibling, this arm's exposure is evidence quality rather than wrong-direction pharmacology. Realistic worst case is irritation or contact sensitisation: three-day forearm patch test first, stop on visible perifollicular darkening, blotching or persistent erythema. The same mandatory anti-melanogenic co-exposure exclusion applies to both cohorts - no topical melatonin, curcumin/tetrahydrocurcumin, N-acetylcysteine or thiol lighteners, hydroquinone, arbutin, kojic, azelaic or tranexamic acid for 4 weeks before and throughout - because melatonin antagonises alpha-MSH-induced tyrosinase accumulation and would silently null both arms.
Novelty rationale
The material change is a change of role, and it reverses a decision made for a reason that no longer holds. H064 deleted this peptide as a duplicate; this version reinstates it as the replication control, because the run's own contra-audit and reviews subsequently established that non-independence of the evidence - not mechanism, not safety, not direction - is the surviving arm's binding constraint, and a second molecule from a different manufacturer at the same receptor is the one clinical design that can test source-dependence directly. That reframing brings a pre-declared interpretation rule with it, including the outcome that is normally never written down in advance: a result that is positive for one molecule only is scored as evidence against the class and against the supplier dataset. It simultaneously repairs the three concrete defects the H051 reviewer named - no vehicle control, no blinded reader, and a retroauricular site that may contain no grey at all - and it permanently strips the cross-molecule evidence transfer from PMID 30222197 that the same reviewer called out, by making that paper the hypothesis under test rather than a source of support. Parallel cohorts rather than a third square on one scalp is also a deliberate correction of the crossover problem that sank the stacked and hemi-scalp designs earlier in the run.
Reviews

No reviews.

5. formal retirement of the topical melatonin + tetrahydrocurcumin redox arm on wrong-direction evidence, converted into a mandatory anti-melanogenic co-exposure exclusion and 4-week washout applied to every surviving arm in this run (H081) Novel candidate androgen

ChEMBL: n/a · Elo 1570 · confidence 5/5 · route: not applicable - arm retired; the surviving deliverable is a topical co-exposure exclusion and 4-week washout applied to the other arms · origin: evolution (round 6) · theme: The redox arm, which led…

Mechanism
This evolution is a kill, and the kill is the deliverable. The parent proposed a two-agent leave-on serum aimed at a real lesion - the millimolar peroxide burden and near-absent catalase/MSRA-B of the grey follicle - on the logic that melatonin would scavenge and tetrahydrocurcumin would restore enzymatic clearance through Nrf2. The round-6 contra-evidence audit searched both components separately and found that both are documented melanogenesis inhibitors sold as skin-whitening actives: tetrahydrocurcumin inhibits tyrosinase activity and protein dose-dependently and is explicitly proposed and marketed as a whitening cosmetic active, and suppresses alpha-MSH-induced melanogenesis via GSK3beta; melatonin and its metabolites inhibit proliferation and tyrosinase activity in human epidermal melanocytes from nanomolar concentrations through MT1/MT2, suppress melanogenesis in human hair follicles specifically, and act partly through the same Nrf2/PI3K-AKT-GSK3 arm the hypothesis invoked as its therapeutic mechanism. The formulation is therefore not merely unproven, it is oriented to remove pigment from the still-pigmented follicles it was designed to protect, and the Nrf2 rationale is itself an anti-melanogenic axis. There is a second, sharper consequence that makes this more than a deletion. Melatonin blocks alpha-MSH-induced tyrosinase accumulation, so it directly antagonises the MC1R biomimetic arm that is the only surviving candidate pointing the right way. Melatonin scalp lotions are widely sold OTC for hair, exactly the population these trials recruit from, and nothing in any prior protocol asked about them. So the retired arm is converted into a screening instrument: every surviving topical and oral arm acquires an explicit exclusion and washout - no topical melatonin (and a recorded question about oral melatonin, which is not excluded but is a covariate), no curcumin or tetrahydrocurcumin topicals, no N-acetylcysteine or other thiol lighteners, no hydroquinone, arbutin, kojic acid, azelaic acid or tranexamic acid on the scalp - for 4 weeks before baseline and throughout, verified by product-label review at each visit rather than by a yes/no question, since consumers do not know their serum contains a whitening active.
Evidence
The direction-reversing evidence is external to this run's 542-record corpus and its provenance is stated rather than dressed up: it comes from the round-6 contra-evidence audit's targeted searches, which named a 2025 Biochemistry Research International paper positioning tetrahydrocurcumin as a melanogenesis inhibitor and skin-whitening active, a 2019 Toxicol Environ Health Sci paper on tetrahydrocurcumin suppressing alpha-MSH-induced melanogenesis via GSK3beta, a Mol Cell Endocrinol study of melatonin metabolites inhibiting melanocyte tyrosinase from 1e-9 M, the 2022 J Pineal Res reassessment of melatonin in melanocyte physiology, and the melatonin/alpha-MSH tyrosinase antagonism (PMID 7556159) supplied by the audit. None of those identifiers were verifiable in this run's corpus, so none is listed in source_refs and none is asserted as corpus-grounded; they are recorded as external contra-evidence, which is the honest status. What IS corpus-verified is everything the retired arm was aiming at, and it is worth preserving because it is what the surviving arm inherits: PMID 19237503 for millimolar H2O2, near-absent catalase and MSRA/B and Met374 sulfoxidation limiting tyrosinase kinetics; PMID 24033376 for the broader oxidative-stress account of greying; PMID 32549695 for systemic oxidative-stress markers in premature canities, which supports the general redox story and, as its own reviewer noted, no particular molecule; PMID 33598985 for H2O2 and noradrenaline driving ectopic melanocyte differentiation in ex vivo human follicles; PMID 33320376 for the alpha-MSH/MC1R/cAMP/MITF axis that melatonin antagonises; PMID 30222197 for the surviving arm the exclusion is written to protect. The conclusion is that a real lesion does not license any agent aimed at it: two agents with impeccable antioxidant credentials turned out to be tyrosinase inhibitors, and the run spent five rounds on them without asking. The generalisable rule extracted here is the audit's own: for a pigment indication, every candidate and every component must be searched for depigmenting/whitening positioning and for depigmenting metabolites before it is scored, not after. (refs: PMID 19237503, PMID 24033376, PMID 32549695, PMID 33598985, PMID 33320376, PMID 30222197)
Safety
Nothing is dosed, so there is no toxicological exposure to discuss; the safety gain is that a plausible route to iatrogenic depigmentation is closed. The specific hazard being retired is not systemic toxicity - both agents are benign in that sense - but the possibility of accelerating the loss of pigment in the still-pigmented follicles a subject came in with, which for a cosmetic indication with zero tolerance for harm is the worst available outcome and would not have been detected by any safety monitoring in the parent protocol, because the endpoint and the harm are the same variable moving in opposite directions. The washout also protects trial validity: an undeclared melatonin scalp lotion in a subject randomised to the MC1R arm produces a null through pharmacological antagonism that would be misattributed to the peptide. Practical implementation: subjects bring every scalp product to the baseline visit for label review; anything containing melatonin, curcuminoids, thiols, hydroquinone, arbutin, kojic, azelaic or tranexamic acid is withdrawn for 4 weeks before enrolment.
Novelty rationale
A clean kill is the material change, and it is not a rewording of anything: the parent's entire content was a two-agent serum plus a hemi-scalp protocol, and both agents are deleted on evidence that neither the parent nor any of its five ancestors ever looked for. What is added in their place is new and was absent from every hypothesis in this run: a screening rule that treats depigmenting cosmetic actives as a confounder and a pharmacological antagonist rather than as background noise, with label-level verification because subjects cannot self-report an ingredient they do not know they are using. Retiring this arm also removes the run's most seductive failure pattern - a correctly identified lesion (peroxide, catalase, Met374) used to license agents chosen for antioxidant credentials without checking their pigment direction - and the H2O2 target is not abandoned, it is left to the one surviving arm that has follicle-derived-cell catalase data and a pro-pigment direction. Finally it forecloses a specific, foreseeable error that would otherwise be attractive on paper: combining melatonin's redox story with the MC1R agonist's melanogenic push. That combination must never be built, because melatonin blocks alpha-MSH-induced tyrosinase accumulation and the two actives cancel at the same axis.

No reviews.

6. palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 (Greyverse) 2% leave-on scalp solution, run as the run's single MC1R arm with an instrumental shaft-melanin endpoint, a subject-level mixed-effects analysis, an operationalised staging gate, and a non-supplier, pre-registered analysis pipeline (H078) Novel candidate —

ChEMBL: n/a · Elo 1569 · confidence 3/5 · route: topical (leave-on solution, vehicle-controlled paired clipped squares) · origin: evolution (round 6) · theme: The only surviving molecular candidates…

Mechanism
The pharmacology is unchanged from H064 and is deliberately not re-argued: palmitoyl tetrapeptide-20 is an alpha-MSH biomimetic that engages MC1R, a Gs-coupled receptor whose cAMP/PKA/CREB output drives MITF and the MITF-dependent melanogenic cassette (TYR, TYRP1, DCT, MLANA), and the target is the anagen hair bulb pigmentary unit rather than the bulge, because human greying invariably begins as declining melanogenesis in the anagen bulb - reduced tyrosinase activity, defective melanosome transfer to precortical matrix keratinocytes, apoptosis of pigmentary-unit melanocytes - with bulge stem-cell depletion the later event that makes greying irreversible (PMID 32965076, PMID 38099887). The single claim is that in follicles still in the reversible, output-deficient stage, MC1R agonism raises pigment output per anagen, visible as a pigmented proximal segment beneath an unpigmented distal shaft. What materially changes is not the molecule and not the mechanism but the two things the round-6 review identified as the arm's actual weak points: the evidence is non-independent, and the readout was subjective and statistically mis-specified. Both are fixed here as design objects rather than as sentences. First, independence: this protocol is specified to be run and analysed without the ingredient house that sells the active, with active and vehicle bottled by an independent compounder, peptide identity and content confirmed by LC-MS on the supplied lots, the statistical analysis plan and primary endpoint pre-registered before first application, and publication committed regardless of direction. That is what converts the arm from a fifth restatement of a supplier claim into the first non-supplier test of it. Second, the readout: blinded human band-calling on 50 hairs per square is replaced by an instrument. Cut shafts are scanned by transmitted-light microdensitometry along their length to give a continuous per-hair pigment profile, from which the proximal-band boundary is located by algorithm rather than by eye, with a subset of hairs subjected to alkaline solubilisation and spectrophotometric melanin quantitation as an orthogonal continuous measure. Human graders are retained only for adjudication of profiles the algorithm flags as ambiguous, with 20% double-read and a pre-declared kappa of at least 0.8.
Evidence
The intervention evidence is stated at its true strength and no higher. PMID 30222197 is the only primary paper and it is in vitro and ex vivo pharmacology: catalase induction and the ~30% fall in intracellular H2O2 are in Human Follicle Dermal Papilla Cells, a fibroblast-lineage population that is neither the bulb melanocyte nor the bulge, so that datum is retained only as proof the peptide is bioactive in follicle-derived cells and is NOT used as a redox mechanism for pigment; the melanogenic data (increased melanin production, increased MC1-R, TRP-1, TRP-2 and Melan-A, reduced ASIP in vitro and in microdissected ex vivo human hairs) carry the pigment claim; and the 15-volunteer arm measured MC1-R protein on plucked hairs after three months of a 10 ppm lotion with no placebo, which is target engagement, not hair colour. PMID 37034550 is carried as a red flag rather than support: >90% grey-to-black conversion at five months in a 25-year-old on concurrent oral biotin and pantothenate is not compatible with ~1 cm/month growth on 15-30 cm shafts. Beyond that, the human evidence for colour with this active is zero, and every substantive datum traces to one supplier-authored source with no independent replication - which is precisely why this version's contribution is an independent, instrumented, pre-registered test rather than another agent. The lesion the arm targets is corpus-anchored: PMID 31316609 shows global downregulation of pigmentation-associated genes in grey versus black follicles of the same premature-greying individuals, i.e. a transcriptional deficit of exactly the kind an MITF-raising agonist could in principle relieve; PMID 34700322 measures TYR, TYRP1 and GP100 across follicle segments in premature greying; PMID 19237503 and PMID 24033376 supply the oxidative context (millimolar H2O2, near-absent catalase and MSRA/B, Met374 sulfoxidation limiting tyrosinase kinetics). Scope limits are explicit: PMID 33382172 shows MC1R is dispensable for acute stress-induced greying in mice, so this arm must not be expected to touch the stress axis, and PMID 25219681 notes MC1R is highly polymorphic, so loss-of-function variants are a plausible non-response mechanism and MC1R genotype is collected as a pre-specified covariate. PMID 36853923 gives the exclusion rule for fully white zones. Design numbers, which the reviewer correctly said were asserted rather than shown: 40 subjects (raised from 24) with early-to-mid canities; two clipped 2 cm squares at least 5 cm apart in the mid-parietal/vertex region, which is where premature greying actually is (vertex 42.5% in PMID 36386090; temporal 35.2%, frontal 18.3% in PMID 28584374) and which is concealable; side randomised; identical vehicle on the control square; separate labelled applicators; 50 hairs cut per square at 6 and 9 months. The analysis unit is the SUBJECT, not the hair: a mixed-effects logistic model with a random intercept per subject and a pre-declared within-subject ICC assumption of 0.2, with paired sides as the contrast; hair-level pseudoreplication is prohibited in the analysis plan. Pre-registered minimum interesting effect: a 15 percentage-point absolute difference in banded-hair proportion. The staging gate is given a number instead of the word 'majority': a square qualifies only if the white-shaft fraction counted on a 1 cm2 trichoscopic grid at 20x by two independent graders falls between 15% and 50%, with disagreement beyond 10 percentage points resolved by a third count. (refs: PMID 30222197, PMID 32965076, PMID 38099887, PMID 31316609, PMID 34700322, PMID 19237503, PMID 24033376, PMID 37034550, PMID 33382172, PMID 25219681, PMID 36853923, PMID 36386090, PMID 28584374)
Safety
Cosmetic-grade OTC leave-on peptide; no prescriber, no drug interactions, negligible systemic absorption of a lipid-conjugated tetrapeptide at 2% on two 2 cm squares. The round-6 contra-evidence audit searched this molecule specifically - supplier and INCI positioning, cosmetic patents, and the mechanistic class - and found no melanogenesis-inhibiting, depigmenting or leukotrichia signal anywhere; its hydrolysis products are palmitic acid and short peptide fragments, neither pigment-inhibiting. So the risk here is evidence quality, not wrong-direction pharmacology. Realistic worst case is local irritation or contact sensitisation to peptide or vehicle preservatives: three-day forearm patch test first. As a melanogenesis stimulant it can in principle darken treated skin as well as hair; squares are sited under overlying hair so any change is concealed, and treatment stops on visible perifollicular darkening, blotching or persistent erythema. Subjects with many atypical naevi get a baseline skin check, which also covers the weak, confounded systemic-melanotan naevus signal noted in the audit. MANDATORY CO-EXPOSURE EXCLUSION, new in this version and load-bearing: no topical melatonin, curcumin or tetrahydrocurcumin, N-acetylcysteine or other thiol lighteners, hydroquinone, arbutin, kojic acid, azelaic or tranexamic acid on the scalp during the trial and for 4 weeks before it. Melatonin in particular blocks alpha-MSH-induced tyrosinase accumulation (PMID 7556159 - supplied by the round-6 contra-evidence audit; not present in this run's 542-record corpus, so cited as external and kept out of source_refs), which means it does not merely fail on its own but pharmacologically antagonises this arm at the same axis. Programme stop rule unchanged and binding: if the pre-registered subject-level analysis shows less than a 15-point difference at 9 months, stop - do not extend, do not stack further actives, do not re-analyse subgroups.
Novelty rationale
No novelty is claimed at the compound level and none exists - Greyverse is the marketed incumbent named in the dossier. Four things materially change from H064, all of them the specific defects its reviewer listed. (1) The endpoint stops being a human eye: microdensitometric shaft profiling plus solubilised-melanin quantitation replaces subjective band-calling, which removes the failure mode where grader drift and expectation are indistinguishable from a small real effect on a high-expectation cosmetic outcome. (2) The statistics are fixed rather than asserted: the reviewer showed that 50 hairs from each of 24 subjects is 24 independent units, not 1200, and that analysing at the hair level would manufacture significance; the analysis unit is now the subject, the model is specified, an ICC is declared, and n rises to 40. (3) Independence is engineered rather than lamented. The single most-cited weakness of this arm across two rounds and the contra-audit is that all of its melanogenic evidence traces to one supplier-authored paper; a pre-registered, non-supplier-run, LC-MS-authenticated, publish-regardless protocol is the only design change that can actually retire that criticism, and it is worth more than adding a second agent. (4) The staging gate becomes an operational threshold (15-50% white-shaft fraction on a counted grid) instead of the unusable word 'majority', and MC1R genotype is collected as a pre-specified non-response covariate on PMID 25219681, which no version of this arm had. The anti-melanogenic co-exposure exclusion is also new and is a direct consequence of the round-6 contra-audit: the retired melatonin and tetrahydrocurcumin arms are not merely dropped, their agents are now banned from the trial because they antagonise the surviving one.
Reviews

No reviews.
